right, well before the toggle — already drops the count to zero and ends the room on the spot.
Confirmed directly: every "hold right" trace won at column ~12 with gravity never once flipping, the
entire toggle/drift/bounce/goal sequence skipped. The brief's "single collectible" was clearly meant
as a mid-run bait item, not the room's real win condition (the goal tile, reached via the ricochet,
plainly is) — fixed with a second gem placed inside the goal area itself, so the center-room pickup
can no longer complete the set alone; gathering it and touching goal both still independently win,
by design, they just can't be satisfied by the bait alone anymore.

**A second real bug, found by the same trace: a chaser catch sent the player to (0,0), not the
room's entrance.** `createEmptyRoom` defaults `startPos` to `{x:0,y:0}`, and the only thing that
normally overwrites it is placing a player directly into a room via the palette
(`placeNewActor`'s own `if (isPlayer) startPos.x = ...`) — which never happens here, since the
player is placed once in Room 1 and only ever *arrives* in Room 2 via the door. Left at (0,0), a
chaser catch (`resolveActorContact`'s own "teleport to `startPos`" respawn) sent the player to open
space above the room's own ceiling line — confirmed directly by trace, a real, nonsensical respawn
spot, not a death (lives remained). Fixed by setting `room2.startPos` explicitly to the door's own
drop-off cell, matching what a hand-placed player would have set. Worth noting for any future
multi-room sample where the player only ever *arrives* via a door rather than being placed by
hand: `startPos` needs the same explicit treatment every time, not just here.

**A third real finding: the Chaser's first placement (7 tiles past the toggle) made the dodge an
almost-guaranteed catch, not a genuine race.** `NPC_MAX_SPEED` (160) closes a 7-tile gap in about
1.4s, while a real measured run from the door to the toggle takes roughly 4.5-5s — so a chaser that
close reaches the toggle's own airspace long before the player ever does. Confirmed by trace: every
attempt at the post-flip diagonal climb got caught within ~150ms of crossing the toggle, every time,
turning "a reactive dodge" into a foregone conclusion. Pulled back to col 37 (near the room's own
right wall, roughly 20 tiles from the toggle) instead — closer to matching the player's own real
run-up time, so the two arrive in the toggle's neighborhood at comparable moments and the dodge
reads as a genuine close-run thing. Re-verified: a plain "hold right, never press up" pass now
floats cleanly along the floor well past the toggle without an immediate catch — a legitimate
"clean float-and-retry" outcome, not a near-certainty of failure.

**The ceiling bounce itself was confirmed working correctly — a prior read of it as "not
bouncing" was a test-and-input artifact, not an engine bug.** `resolveAxis`'s bouncy-reflection
branch for a ceiling hit from below (`actor.vy < 0`) is not gated on `gravityOn()` — only the
player-only "wall kick" bonus is — so the base restitution reflection fires in free-roam exactly
like it does under gravity. A live-key trace that held "up" continuously through and past the
bounce showed the player appearing to just slide along the ceiling's underside instead of
rebounding — confirmed, via an isolated teleport-with-known-velocity test (after first discovering
and fixing a mistake in that same test: it was checking a position before the room-transition
animation, `ROOM_TRANSITION_MS = 420`, had actually cleared, so `tick()` was still frozen and
nothing moved at all), that the raw reflection genuinely fires (`vy` flips from strongly negative to
strongly positive on contact) — it's just that continuing to hold "up" through the bounce means the
player's own `MOVE_ACCEL` thrust immediately out-accelerates the brief rebound back toward negative
again, which is the correct, intuitive physics outcome of pushing into a surface rather than letting
it carry you away from it. A kid has to actually let go of (or reverse) the climbing key at the
bounce for the rebound to read as a rebound, which is exactly the "reactive, not autopilot" feel the
whole chain is built around — not every input pattern should produce the same outcome.

**A fourth real finding, downstream of the one above: a single goal tile at a hand-guessed landing
spot was unreachable, the same knife-edge shape Worst Elevator's own goal placement already hit
once.** Traced directly: holding right+up into the bounce, then releasing up and holding down to
actually descend (rather than just hug the ceiling), produces a clean 45° dive from around the
bounce point (col ~22.6) that crosses row 9 at column ~24.9 — nowhere near a single hand-placed
target. Rather than refining the guess, the goal was widened into a whole row (`fillGravRect(board,
23, 9, 29, 9, "goal", "none")`, columns 23-29) — the exact fix that worked for Worst Elevator's own
goal placement, bracketing the traced crossing with real margin for a shallower or steeper dive.
Deliberately placed at row 9, well above the floor's own resting height (row ~13, where a player who
never bounces at all ends up floating once the pop washes out) — a goal planted at floor height
would let the "never press up" float-along-the-floor outcome reach it too, trivializing the one
route that's actually supposed to require the bounce; confirmed directly that it doesn't. The
second bait-fix gem (above) was moved to sit inside this same row rather than at a separate column,
so it's gathered naturally the moment a successful dive lands, not a second target to aim for.

**Phase 2 checklist, each confirmed directly rather than assumed:**
- **Spikes still hurt an airborne, non-grounded actor, not just a grounded one** — confirmed by a
  sustained zero-g drift straight into a row-11 spike cell: `hazardCooldown` jumps from 0 to a real
  positive value exactly on contact, the same reaction a grounded walk-in gets.
- **The Chaser gets the same gravity-off event and pop as the player, not special-cased out** —
  confirmed directly: the Chaser's own `vy` reads negative (upward) immediately after the flip, from
  the same plain `actors.forEach` loop in `triggerFlip` that pops every actor in the room.
- **The toggle is genuinely scoped to this room only** — confirmed directly: flipping gravity in
  Room 2 leaves Room 1's own stored `gravityEnabled` untouched (`true` before and after), the
  natural consequence of the "hot globals" per-room architecture, not something this patch had to
  build — but worth confirming rather than trusting the architecture at face value, per this
  project's own history of specced behavior not always being fully wired.

**Deliberately embraced, not eliminated, per the brief's own framing**: a drift that floats along
the floor and meets the Chaser, or one that climbs into the spike ring instead of the bounce, are
both treated as fair, intended alternate outcomes — "sometimes a perfect ricochet, sometimes a
spike, sometimes a clean float-and-retry" is the actual target, not a single guaranteed beat. No
code or layout decision in this patch tries to make the ricochet the only reachable outcome.

**Visuals**: a floor/ceiling texture pair (`GRAV_FLOOR_ART`, a warm earthy orange; `GRAV_CEIL_ART`,
a cool blue) gives a kid a visceral "which way is down" cue before and after the flip, per the
brief's own light ask — not the focus of this sample the way art was for the last two, just a small
functional touch. The one bouncy ceiling segment (`GRAV_PANEL_ART`) is a visually loud yellow/orange
outlier against the plain blue ceiling flanking it, continuing the established "look vs. consequence
must be visually distinct" rule from Spin Cycle's own soap-chunk/suds-patch pairing and the v2.41
caption-bar work.

**No engine code changed beyond the one new constant** (`GRAVITY_OFF_POP_SPEED`, plus its one line
in `triggerFlip` and its `constantsSource` export line) — every other piece (Button, spikes, the
bouncy modifier, goal, collectible) is an existing, already-validated mechanic; this sample's whole
contribution is the pop, the level content, and the string of real layout bugs the trace-and-fix
pass above caught.

Verified with Playwright across many focused passes rather than one broad one, matching the room's
own "light verification burden" framing: the bouncyCooldown runaway found and fixed; the duplicate
player placement (room 1 left with zero actors) found and fixed; the row-13 spike layout blocking
the only walking path found and fixed (moved to row 11, confirmed by trace that a standing actor's
own height never reaches it while grounded); the premature full-collection win found and fixed; the
(0,0) chaser-catch respawn found and fixed; the too-close Chaser found and fixed with a re-traced
"clean float, no immediate catch" control case; the isolated ceiling-bounce reflection confirmed
working (after finding and fixing a room-transition-timing mistake in the test itself); the
widened-goal-row dive confirmed landing a win; spikes-hurt-while-airborne, chaser-pop, and
per-room-scoping each confirmed directly; the full 8-sample regression sweep (all prior samples plus
this one) and a dedicated export test (door transition, toggle flip, both confirmed working in a
freshly exported standalone game) all run error-free; and a final debug-hook-free sanity pass
(`grep -n "toyDebug" index.html` returning nothing) confirmed clean.

## Unify physics-object interactions: one shared category, not per-mechanic allow-lists

A refactor of existing, proven mechanics, treated accordingly — careful, isolated, verified
against the full existing sample suite before and after, not mixed into other feature work. Direct
report: bombs don't bounce off trampolines despite the v2.28 "widened to physics objects" patch;
most objects can't be loaded into a cannon; most objects can't be affected by wind. The actual
shape of the problem, confirmed by reading every gate before touching any of them: bounce
(`CARRYABLE_KINDS`), cannon-loading (`STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS`), and magnet (a narrow inline
`pushable-or-collectible` check) each maintained their own separate allow-list of which actor kinds
they applied to — every time a new object type was added, someone had to remember to update every
list individually, and missing one left a silent, uncaught gap, the default outcome rather than the
exception.

**The fix, generalizing `CARRYABLE_KINDS`'s own existing pattern rather than building a parallel
system**: `PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS` (`CARRYABLE_KINDS.concat([HAZARD_BALL_KIND])`) is the one shared
answer to "does this actor kind have a genuine physics body" — every carryable (already a full
position/velocity/collision body) plus the hazard ball (same, just never hand-placed or carryable).
`isForceSusceptible(temperament)` is the default-in gate the four optional forces — bounce/
trampoline reflection, cannon loading, wind/fan force, magnet pull — now all read from:
`PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS.includes(temperament) && temperament !== LARGE_STONE_KIND`. A new object type
gets all four for free the moment it's defined, the opposite of needing to be individually wired
into four separate places.

**Deliberately NOT extended to characters.** `isForceSusceptible` does not include `wanderer`/
`chaser`/`fleer`/`stationary` — those are CHARACTERS, not OBJECTS, and keep their own fixed
reaction table (`resolveActorContact`). "NPCs only ever react to geometry, never to modifiers" (the
v2.28 bounce-widening patch's own stated line) is an existing, deliberate design decision this
refactor does not move — the reported gaps and the request itself were both about objects
specifically (bomb, "most objects," cannon-loadable things), never about a wandering NPC's own
reactions.

**The one legitimate exception: Large Stone.** Already built heavy and lethal-immune for the
bridge-building puzzle use case (v2.34) — "too heavy to be pushed around by wind, launched by a
cannon, bounced by a trampoline, or dragged by a magnet" is the same identity, coherently extended,
not a gap. `isForceSusceptible` carves it out by name. Resist adding more exceptions as new object
types arrive — a thing that seems to need one is a prompt to ask whether it's really in Large
Stone's weight class, not a reason to let exceptions multiply the way the original per-mechanic
gaps did.

**Gravity gets no type-based exemption, not even for Large Stone — confirmed already true, not
something this patch had to build.** Wind/cannon/bounce/magnet are optional forces layered on top
of baseline weight; gravity isn't one of them, it's what gives "heavy" its meaning. Read through
`stepActor`'s own gravity branch before writing anything: it was already fully kind-agnostic, with
exactly the three contextual (not identity-based) exemptions the task asked for already in place —
actively carried (`stepActor`'s own early `if (actor.carried) return`), stuck to a sticky wall
(`isStuckToSticky`, itself also already kind-agnostic, no list of any kind), and a room with gravity
globally off. Nothing needed changing here; this section exists to document that it was checked, not
assumed.

**What actually changed, mechanically** (every call site found by grep, not assumed complete):
- **Bounce**: `resolveAxis`'s `isPhysicsObject` and `bounceOffTrampolineObjects`'s
  `actorIsPhysicsObject` both switched from `CARRYABLE_KINDS.includes(...)` to
  `isForceSusceptible(...)` — Large Stone is the only thing this actually removes (it was already
  in `CARRYABLE_KINDS`); hazard ball's own separate, pre-existing "always bounces off every wall"
  rule (`isHazardBall`, unconditional, unrelated to the bouncy modifier) is untouched and still
  OR'd in alongside it.
- **Cannon loading**: both of `triggerCannon`'s trigger-gating call sites (`resolveAxis`'s own
  collision-time trigger, and the build-mode drag handler's `fireBlockingTileReaction`) switched
  from `STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS.includes(...)` to `isForceSusceptible(...)` — this is the real fix
  for "most objects can't be loaded into a cannon": a hazard ball, or a dropped/thrown magnet,
  Trampoline object, bomb2, or cannon2, can now trigger a cannon it's shoved or thrown into, the
  same as a pushed crate always could. `STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS` itself is untouched and still used
  exactly as before for `shovePushables`/`actorBlockedByOtherPushables`/the crush-on-fall-from-above
  reaction — "can other actors stand on or be blocked by this" is a different physical trait than
  any of the four forces this refactor unifies, not one of the five named mechanics, and out of
  scope.
- **Magnet**: `radialForceAccelFor`'s `magnetable` switched from a narrow `temperament ===
  PUSHABLE_KIND || temperament === "collectible"` check to `isForceSusceptible(temperament) ||
  temperament === "collectible"` — collectible is kept as its own explicit OR rather than folded
  into `PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS`, since a collectible has no driven physics body at all (`stepActor`'s
  own `stationary`/`collectible` early-return skips the entire branch that computes
  `radialForceAccelFor` in the first place) — this was already dead/unreachable code before this
  patch and stays exactly as dormant after it, not something this refactor is scoped to fix.
- **Wind/fan**: these never had a per-kind allow-list at all — confirmed by reading `windAccelFor`/
  `fanAccelFor` before writing anything, both are purely positional and were already folded into
  every non-stationary/collectible actor's `ax`/`ay` unconditionally, characters included. So
  "most objects can't be affected by wind" wasn't actually a code gap to widen — the fix here is
  narrower than the other three: a single `isLargeStone` subtraction in `stepActor` zeroes both
  accels for Large Stone specifically, rather than a new default-in check for everyone else (who
  already had it).
- **Sticky cling**: `isStuckToSticky` needed no change at all — already kind-agnostic, confirmed by
  reading it, not assumed.

**Verification, scaled to how foundational this is.** Confirmed directly (not inferred from the
code read alone): a thrown bomb2 bounces off both a Trampoline TILE and a dropped Trampoline
OBJECT actor; a hazard ball bumped into a cannon2 tile triggers it (`cannonCooldown` set); a magnet
tile now pulls a hazard ball that sat still before (no regression on the existing pushable/
collectible pull); wind still blows a hazard ball exactly as before (confirming the "already
kind-agnostic" finding rather than assuming it); Large Stone is now confirmed immune to all four —
no bounce, no cannon-firing, no wind push, no magnet pull — while still falling under gravity with
no exemption and still landing/resting normally. The concrete payoff named in the request was
verified end to end, not left as a theoretical consequence: a Large Stone pushed into a `none:lethal`
tile (lava) falls in, settles, and survives — `checkPhysicsObjectLethalContacts`'s own existing
`PUSHABLE_KIND`-only scope (v2.34, Large Stone deliberately excluded) already made this possible
before this patch; this refactor's own contribution was confirming it actually works as a real
emergent mechanic, not assuming it from reading two functions' scopes in isolation.

**Regression risk, assessed directly rather than just re-running every sample and hoping.** Grepped
every built-in sample's own board-building source before trusting the change was safe: no sample
places a `magnet` tile anywhere, and no sample places a Large Stone actor anywhere — meaning both
the widened and the newly-narrowed halves of this refactor touch zero existing sample content by
construction, not just by a passing test run. The bounce widening to hazard ball is redundant with
hazard ball's own pre-existing always-bounce rule (no behavior change there either). The cannon
widening is the one genuinely new category of interaction an existing sample could exercise (a
hazard ball or dropped carryable now able to trigger a cannon it never could before) — confirmed
directly that The World's Worst Elevator, the one sample that chains cannon with the most other
mechanics, still completes its full intended sequence (fall → wind → bounce → platform → switch →
gate → cannon → door → trampoline → goal) end to end with zero errors, in both the live editor and
a freshly exported standalone game. The full 8-sample regression sweep (Cheese Heist, Tunnel
Trouble, Dungeon Diving, The Monster's Den, The World's Worst Elevator, Spin Cycle, The Catapult
Factory, The Gravity Switcheroo) each load, enter Play, and run a real held-right session with zero
console errors.

**Export pipeline**: `PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS` got its own `constantsSource` line; `isForceSusceptible`
was added to `runtimeFunctions`. Every function this change touches
(`resolveAxis`, `bounceOffTrampolineObjects`, `radialForceAccelFor`, `stepActor`, the drag handler's
`fireBlockingTileReaction`) was already shared into the export from earlier patches, so no new
export-side gaps were introduced — confirmed directly: a fresh export of The World's Worst Elevator
contains both new identifiers and completes its full playthrough in the exported file too.

Verified with Playwright (19 targeted checks plus the full regression sweep and export test): bomb-
off-trampoline-tile, bomb-off-trampoline-object, hazard-ball-triggers-cannon, wind-still-affects-
hazard-ball, magnet-now-pulls-hazard-ball, Large-Stone-immune-to-wind, Large-Stone-immune-to-
magnet, Large-Stone-still-falls-under-gravity, Large-Stone-sinks-into-lava-and-survives, the full
8-sample regression sweep, The World's Worst Elevator's complete sequence replayed end to end in
both the live editor and a fresh export, and a final debug-hook-free sanity pass (`grep -n
"toyDebug" index.html` returning nothing).

## Moving platforms (riding, not a new piece) and NPCs joining physics with a recovery window

Two extensions of the "Unify physics-object interactions" patch directly above, requested as a
single follow-up right after that one shipped — treated with the same rigor (investigate before
building, verify directly, full regression sweep, no retroactive content fixes beyond what's
asked).

### Riding: a wide pushable or Large Stone on a conveyor is now a real moving platform

Direct correction of an earlier claim from the physics-object-unification pass: "a pushable block
on a conveyor already moves" is true, but is a different claim from "standing on top of something
carries you along with it." Investigated before writing anything: `shovePushables`'s own vy>0
branch (landing on a pushable from above) already sets the rider's `grounded = true`, but never
touched the rider's position relative to the platform on any SUBSEQUENT frame — once resting, a
rider's own position only ever moved from its OWN velocity, while the platform underneath
continued drifting via `windAccelFor`'s existing cellsUnderneath force. The two would simply drift
apart; nothing ever carried a rider along.

**Fixed generally, in `tick()`, not per-mechanic**: right before the per-actor `stepActor` loop
runs each frame, every `STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS` actor (pushable, Large Stone) is checked for
riders — any OTHER actor whose bottom edge sits within `RIDE_CONTACT_EPSILON` (3px) of its top
edge, with horizontal overlap — using THIS frame's starting positions, before anyone has moved yet
(checking post-step positions would already reflect the very drift this exists to correct). After
the loop finishes, each platform's actual frame delta (`platform.x/y` now vs. its recorded
pre-step position) is applied as a direct position offset to every one of its recorded riders — the
same "set position directly" approach `tick()`'s own carried-object follow uses just above it, not
a velocity trick `applyAxisFriction` would fight. **Single level only**: a rider stacked two deep
(riding a platform that's itself riding another) won't get the fully compounded shift in the same
frame — the same "low-risk generalization, not a full physics simulation" scope this file's own
pushable-stacking work already accepted, and the only case actually asked for (a tile conveys the
platform; the platform conveys whoever's standing on it) only ever needs one level.

**Once true, a moving platform needs no new palette piece at all** — it's just a wide pushable or
Large Stone sitting on a `solid:wind-*` conveyor tile, already fully buildable from existing
pieces, exactly as the request anticipated ("everything else in this patch becomes free once the
underlying rule is fixed once").

**Direct check requested: does The World's Worst Elevator actually have a true rideable platform
today, or has it been getting by on bounce/wind/cannon chains alone?** Read `buildWorstElevatorGame`
directly rather than guessed: Room 1's "platform" (`fillWorstElevatorRect(board, 13, 14, 21, 14,
"solid", "none")`) is a **plain static floor segment** — `solid:none`, no conveyor, never moves.
Confirmed: the sample has been getting by on bounce/wind/cannon chains alone the whole time, never
a genuinely rideable surface, despite its own name. Not retrofitted in this patch — the request
asked for the general mechanism and a direct check, not a rebuild of an existing shipped sample;
flagging this plainly rather than quietly leaving it unmentioned.

**The wind-direction UI, part 2 — "a clickable edit, instead of rotation."** The conveyor's own
direction used to be set by repeatedly clicking an already-placed wind tile, cycling N→E→S→W one
step per click (the same `cannonRotateStrokeCells` "click again to rotate" trick cannon2/fan/
Gate/Spawner all share). Replaced, for wind specifically, with a direct 4-arrow picker:
`windDirPickerArrowPos`/`windDirPickerHitAt`/`drawWindDirPicker` place one small clickable arrow
glyph at each cardinal edge of a hovered/last-tapped wind tile (reusing `WIND_DIRECTIONS`' own
cardinal vectors, not four hand-placed positions), rendered via the same canvas-primitive
affordance style `drawRotateBadge` (v2.41) already established for Build-mode-only editing cues.
Clicking directly sets the direction (`setWindDirectionAt`) rather than cycling — a kid picks the
exact direction they want in one click instead of guessing how many times to re-click. The old
click-to-cycle branch inside `paintGeometryModifierAt` is now a plain no-op re-confirm on an
already-wind-tagged cell (direction only ever changes via the picker now); painting a FRESH wind
tile is unchanged (still defaults to `WIND_ORDER[0]`). The underlying stored data format
(`"solid:wind-n"` etc.) is completely untouched — this is a pure input-method change, with zero
backward-compat or runtime risk, matching `drawRotateBadge`/`rotateGroundSkinAt`'s own precedent of
being Build-mode-only code never added to `exportStandaloneGame`'s `runtimeFunctions` (an exported
game hardcodes `appMode = "play"`, so the whole picker is statically unreachable there).

**A real bug caught before shipping, not by luck**: the first implementation placed each arrow
exactly `TILE/2 - 8` from the tile's center and used an 8px hit radius — meaning the hit-test
distance and the hit RADIUS were numerically equal, so clicking the tile's exact geometric CENTER
registered as a `<=` match against whichever direction's circle happened to be checked first
(`wind-n`, first in `WIND_ORDER`). A kid clicking the plain middle of an existing conveyor tile —
not aiming at any edge at all — would have silently set it to North. Caught by a Playwright check
that deliberately clicked dead-center and found it returned `"wind-n"` instead of `null`. Fixed by
widening the gap between the two numbers (11px offset, 7px hit radius, leaving a real ~4px dead
zone around the tile's center) rather than just shaving one value — confirmed directly afterward
that a center click now correctly misses all four arrows.

**A second thing confirmed, not assumed**: the mousedown/touchstart handlers only ever hit-test the
wind-picker arrows when the clicked cell's OWN existing tile is already wind-tagged (checked via
`GAMEPLAY_TILE_DEFS` lookup before calling `windDirPickerHitAt` at all) — an ordinary click near the
edge of any OTHER cell elsewhere on the board can never be accidentally swallowed by this new check,
since the geometric arrow positions are only even computed for a cell that's genuinely a wind tile.

### NPCs (wanderer/chaser/fleer) join bounce/cannon/magnet physics — with a recovery window

Not a binary "should NPCs bounce" question, per the request's own framing — it's about who's in
control, and for how long, after a bounce/wind/cannon/magnet event displaces one. Full exemption
(the pre-existing "NPCs only ever react to geometry, never to modifiers" rule — see `resolveAxis`'s
own long-standing comment) would reintroduce exactly the per-mechanic inconsistency the physics-
object unification patch just finished removing, and loses real comedy value the request named
directly — the Gravity Switcheroo sample's own floating-chaser beat already assumes a chaser can be
caught up in physics. Full ragdoll with no recovery was ruled out too: an NPC flung unpredictably
forever becomes an unreadable threat a kid can't reasonably respond to.

**Checked first whether a "don't fight the physics while displaced, resume once settled" mechanism
already existed**, per the request's own explicit instruction, before building anything — re-read
the Gravity Switcheroo's own chaser-pop mechanism (`triggerFlip`'s uncolored branch,
`actors.forEach(a => { a.vy -= GRAVITY_OFF_POP_SPEED; })`, applied to every actor in the room on a
gravity flip) directly rather than trusting its own doc section's "chasers go passive during a
gravity-flip drift" framing at face value. Confirmed: no such mechanism exists there — the chaser's
own steering (`steeringAccelFor`) keeps running completely unsuppressed through the pop; the
"passive" reading in that doc section is describing an EMERGENT effect (the chaser is now also
subject to the same zero-g physics as the player, diluting its menace), not a literal steering-
suppression primitive. The one genuinely close existing precedent is `isStuckToSticky`'s own
`stuck` flag, which already gates OUT the ordinary player-input/NPC-steering contribution entirely
while actively clinging — the right shape to generalize, but narrower in cause (only sticky contact)
than what this ask needs (any of four different triggers).

**Resolution: NPCs become real physics bodies for bounce/cannon/magnet too** (wind/fan already
applied unconditionally to every non-stationary/collectible actor, characters included — confirmed
by reading `windAccelFor`/`fanAccelFor` directly, neither has ever had a kind-gate of any sort, so
this needed no change at all), **but a new `displacedCooldown` field suppresses `steeringAccelFor`'s
own contribution for a short beat (`NPC_DISPLACED_RECOVERY_MS`, 550ms) after whatever triggered
it**, deliberately kept as its own field rather than folded into the existing `bouncyCooldown`/
`cannonCooldown` (those exist specifically to exempt the SPEED-CAP clamp, a different job from
suppressing steering, and reusing them would have coupled two independent concerns). `isForceSusceptible`
and `isSteeringCharacter` stay two SEPARATE, explicit gates (never merged into one list) — a
deliberate choice mirroring the request's own framing: a physics object has no will to hand back,
a steering character does, and that's the entire reason this needs its own mechanism at all.

**Wiring, every call site found by grep before trusting it was complete**: `resolveAxis`'s `bouncy`
flag and `bounceOffTrampolineObjects`'s own copy both gained `isSteeringNPC` alongside
`isPhysicsObject` in their OR chain (and every `bounceSound`-gating condition alongside them, four
in `resolveAxis`, one in `bounceOffTrampolineObjects` — a bounced chaser should be heard, same
reasoning as every other "make it heard, not just felt" widening in this file); both cannon-trigger
sites (`resolveAxis`'s collision-time trigger and the build-mode drag handler's
`fireBlockingTileReaction`) gained the same OR term; `radialForceAccelFor`'s `magnetable` gained it
too, so a magnet genuinely pulls a chaser now, not just suppresses its steering while something
else does the pulling. `displacedCooldown = NPC_DISPLACED_RECOVERY_MS` is set, unconditionally for
any non-player actor (harmless no-op for a pushable/hazard-ball, which has no steering contribution
to suppress in the first place, so no extra gate is needed at the SET sites), at: the bounce branch
in `resolveAxis`, the bounce branch in `bounceOffTrampolineObjects`, and inside `triggerCannon`
itself. Wind/fan/magnet get the SAME field through a different mechanism — a new check in
`stepActor`, right after `windAccel`/`radialAccel`/`fanAccel` are computed, measures their combined
magnitude and RESETS `displacedCooldown` to the full window every single frame it exceeds
`NPC_DISPLACEMENT_FORCE_THRESHOLD` (400 px/s² — comfortably below both `MAGNET_FORCE`'s peak (900)
and `WIND_FORCE` (1000), so ordinary use of either genuinely counts, but a magnet's own far-radius
falloff tail doesn't) — this naturally keeps steering suppressed for the WHOLE time a strong
continuous force is active, then lets the normal per-frame decrement carry the cooldown down over
its own short tail once the force stops, rather than needing a second, parallel mechanism.

**"Resume once settled," not just the fixed timeout** — `stepActor` zeroes `displacedCooldown`
immediately once `gravityOn() && actor.grounded`, right after the per-axis collision calls each
frame. Deliberately gravity-mode-only: `actor.grounded` is unconditionally `true` every frame in
free-roam (this function's own pre-existing comment already says so), so checking it there would
end the window the instant it started; free-roam relies on the fixed timeout alone, which is the
documented, accepted scope boundary for this specific case.

**A genuine test-authoring trap hit twice in a row while verifying this, worth recording**: a
bounce/kick's reflected velocity is real and correct, but `FRICTION_DECEL` (1800) is strong enough
to fully erase a modest reflected speed (the ~131px/s a 150-speed chaser gets off a bouncy wall, or
a manually-set -150 in free-roam) within roughly 70-100ms — checking a bounced/displaced actor's
velocity 150ms after the event reads an already-fully-decayed (but entirely expected) zero as "no
bounce happened" or "steering wasn't actually suppressed," when the real signal was there and gone
well before the check ran. Confirmed by direct frame-by-frame traces
(`trace_chaser_bounce.js`/`trace_freeroam_displaced.js`) in both cases before concluding anything —
checking within the first 20-40ms instead resolved both apparent failures as test-timing mistakes,
not engine bugs. A second, related trap: manually setting a test velocity beyond `NPC_MAX_SPEED`
(160) without ALSO setting the `bouncyCooldown`/`cannonCooldown` exemption a real bounce/cannon
event would naturally carry gets it clamped back to the cap on the very first frame regardless of
`displacedCooldown` — unrelated to steering suppression, but easy to misread as if it were.

**The exaggerated-pose fairness requirement, not optional per the request** — a displaced NPC needs
to be visually obvious while airborne, not a subtle nudge, so a kid can see it coming and react.
`renderActors` adds a continuous spin (`t.rot += (now / 90) * spinFrac`, scaled by how much of
`NPC_DISPLACED_RECOVERY_MS` is left so it winds down as steering returns rather than cutting off
flat) for any non-player `isSteeringCharacter` actor with `displacedCooldown > 0` — a cheap,
reused-from-nowhere-else cue deliberately separate from the existing `impactWobbleValue` squash
(tuned for a quick landing compress, not a sustained "this creature got launched" read). Verified
via debug-hook field inspection and a render-path error check rather than pixel sampling, consistent
with this file's own established tolerance for that distinction on purely cosmetic cues.

**Catch/collision consequences stay completely unchanged regardless of how an NPC arrived** — per
the request's own explicit requirement. `resolveActorContact`'s reaction table was never touched by
this patch at all; a chaser that reaches the player by walking, by being bounced, or by being blown
by wind triggers the exact same catch reaction either way, since the table only ever reads the
contact itself, never how either actor got there.

**Export pipeline**: `RIDE_CONTACT_EPSILON`, `NPC_DISPLACED_RECOVERY_MS`,
`NPC_DISPLACEMENT_FORCE_THRESHOLD` each got `constantsSource` lines; `isSteeringCharacter` was added
to `runtimeFunctions`. `tick`/`stepActor`/`resolveAxis`/`bounceOffTrampolineObjects`/
`radialForceAccelFor`/`triggerCannon`/`renderActors` were all already shared into the export from
earlier patches, so the riding mechanism and the NPC-physics widening both apply automatically to
an exported game with no new gaps — confirmed directly: a fresh export's `GAME_DATA`/runtime
contains every new identifier, and a rider standing on an exported game's own moving platform is
genuinely carried along in the downloaded file, not just the live editor.

Verified with Playwright across several focused passes (29 targeted checks, plus the full 8-sample
regression sweep and a dedicated export test): a crate drifting on a conveyor carries a rider on top
of it along at the same speed, staying in the same relative spot (true riding, not drifting apart);
a chaser bounces off a bouncy wall with a real, code-verified reflected velocity and a set
`displacedCooldown`; a wanderer bumped into a cannon fires it; a magnet now pulls a chaser upward; a
manually-set `displacedCooldown` genuinely suppresses steering (a chaser moving away from the player
stays moving away, not reversed) and steering demonstrably resumes once it expires; landing on an
ordinary floor zeroes a deliberately-huge simulated `displacedCooldown` almost immediately rather
than waiting out the full window; the wind-direction picker's hit-test correctly identifies each of
the four arrows and correctly misses the tile's own center; a plain non-wind tile is never
mistakenly treated as hit-testable; Large Stone remains fully immune to the magnet even after the
NPC widening (confirming the widening is additive, not a loosening of the existing exemption); The
World's Worst Elevator's complete sequence still wins end to end after this patch, in both the live
editor and a fresh export; a real-UI pass with no debug hook at all (genuine mouse clicks through
the actual palette and canvas, screenshotted before and after) confirms the wind-direction picker
visibly renders its four arrows and the highlighted "current direction" arrow genuinely moves from
North to South after clicking the south arrow; the full 8-sample regression sweep (all samples load,
enter Play, and run a held-right session with zero console errors) and a fresh export both pass
clean; and a final debug-hook-free sanity check (`grep -n "toyDebug" index.html` returning nothing)
confirms no test-only code was left behind.

## Moving Platform (from an undeployed sprite-editor icon), and Gravity Well becomes a real black hole

Direct follow-up, two bundled asks right after the riding/NPC-physics patch above shipped: "time to
implement the moving platform from our undeployed sprites... default to moving state (back and
forth). Clicking causes it to go up and down. It's path should be 4 tiles in each direction from
it's initial position," and separately, "Gravity well...this needs to be a more powerful effect
like a black hole... works on all moveable objects including all characters."

**Moving Platform's art is `hazards.MOVING_PLATFORM`** from the sprite-editor tool's own original
24-icon extraction (see the v2.27/v2.36 sections above for that tool's history) — one of the 16
icons that had sat unwired since v2.27's own explicit scope note named it. Extracted and verified
byte-for-byte against the original `ART_DATA` before trusting it (the same "fidelity, not
re-interpretation" process v2.26's monster/skeleton/tabby-cat fix established): a first hand-
transcribed `pat()` row attempt had the support-leg columns off by one, caught by a Python script
comparing the reconstructed array against the original index-by-index, fixed by re-deriving the
rows programmatically rather than guessing again.

**A genuinely new, third `STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS` member, not a re-skinned pushable.**
`MOVING_PLATFORM_KIND`/`MOVING_PLATFORM_DEF_ID` follow the exact same
`OBJECT_ACTOR_KINDS`/`KIND_DEF_ID_OVERRIDES`/`actorDefs`-seeding/`restoreProjectSnapshot`-backfill
shape Large Stone already established — needed its own kind specifically so it could be made
self-driven and immovable while pushable/Large Stone stay shovable.

**Motion**: a fixed triangle-wave oscillation, computed directly in `stepActor` in a new branch
checked BEFORE the stationary/collectible dispatch (so it applies regardless of `isPlayer`, though
nothing but a non-player instance of this kind will ever exist) — no gravity, no steering, no
friction, `vx`/`vy` always read 0. `actor.platformT` (elapsed time) and `actor.platformAxis`
("x" default, "y" after a click) are the only new per-actor fields (harmless/unused on every other
kind, same pattern `carried`/`carrying` already set on this file's own established precedent).
The oscillation center is `spawnX`/`spawnY` — already "wherever this actor was placed," per every
actor's existing field — not a new one. `MOVING_PLATFORM_RANGE_TILES = 4` (the requested "4 tiles
in each direction," an 8-tile total swing) and `MOVING_PLATFORM_SPEED = 70` (px/s along its own
track — not specified by the request, chosen to be slow enough to comfortably stand/ride on and
fast enough to read as purposeful; flag this for tuning if it feels off once played with) feed a
plain triangle-wave formula: `period = 4*range/speed`, phase swept 0→1→0→-1→0 across that period,
multiplied by `range` for the offset added to whichever axis is active.

**Immovable but stand-on-able — a new branch in `shovePushables`, not a reuse of the pushable
displace-then-revert path.** Displacing a self-driven actor by however far the mover just moved
(the existing mechanism) makes no sense for something whose own position is dictated by a formula,
not physics — so a Moving Platform gets its own unconditional "mover clamps flush against my edge"
branch instead, the same shape `bounceOffTrampolineObjects` already uses for a Trampoline object
(which is also "something other actors collide with but never displace"). A rider lands on top
with `grounded = true` exactly like resting on a pushable. **Riding itself needed no new code at
all** — the general "an actor resting on a `STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS` member inherits its own
frame's position delta" mechanism from the immediately-preceding patch (see "Moving platforms via
riding" above) already covers a Moving Platform for free, since it's simply a third member of that
same list; confirmed directly with Playwright rather than assumed from the membership alone.

**Click-to-toggle axis, not drag.** A click on an already-placed Moving Platform (while
`paintMode === "actor"`) flips `platformAxis` between "x" and "y" instead of starting the ordinary
drag-to-reposition gesture every other actor gets — the same tradeoff cannon2/fan/Gate/Spawner
geometry tiles already accept (repainted/rotated from the palette, not dragged) extended to an
actor for the first time. Deliberately does NOT reset `platformT` on toggle — the phase carries
over as-is, so toggling mid-swing produces a position discontinuity (the just-abandoned axis snaps
back to its own `spawnX`/`spawnY` exactly, the newly-active axis picks up wherever that phase value
already put it) rather than a smooth re-center; a reasonable, simple choice, not confirmed with the
user, flagged here in case a smoother re-center on toggle is wanted instead. `resetActorsToSpawn`
also resets `platformT = 0` for this kind specifically (a new, one-line addition, same "the four
places new persistent state must be wired in" discipline the Rooms section's own table teaches) —
without it, "reset to start"/Play entry would snap the platform's position back to spawn only for
the very next frame's own triangle-wave formula to immediately overwrite that reset with wherever
the un-reset phase says it should be, defeating the reset's whole purpose.

**Gravity Well: "a more powerful effect, like a black hole," with the fast/slow distinction made
explicit rather than left to the force curve alone.** `GRAVITY_WELL_FORCE` goes from 900 (same
magnitude as magnet) to 4200 (~4.7x magnet's own peak, ~3x plain gravity), and gains its own
`GRAVITY_WELL_RADIUS_TILES` (7, nearly double magnet's `RADIAL_FORCE_RADIUS_TILES` of 4) — a
separate constant specifically so widening the well's own reach doesn't also change magnet's
established feel as an unrequested side effect. `radialForceAccelFor`'s scan loop now bounds
itself by `Math.max(RADIAL_FORCE_RADIUS_TILES, GRAVITY_WELL_RADIUS_TILES)` and picks a per-source
`radiusPx` (magnet's own narrower one, or the well's wider one) inside that shared scan — a small
restructure, not a rewrite, of the one loop both presets already shared.

**The redirect/capture line is a real, explicit speed threshold — `GRAVITY_WELL_CAPTURE_SPEED =
240`, just above `MAX_SPEED` (220)** — not an emergent side effect of the force curve alone, a
deliberate departure from this file's usual "let simple physics rules produce the behavior"
preference. The request itself named two distinct outcomes in words ("drastically redirects" vs.
"sucks them in and destroys them"), not one continuous effect for a force curve to interpolate
between — and a purely emergent design (relying on "a fast mover only spends a moment in the
field") has a real failure mode here that an explicit threshold avoids: without it, any actor whose
AABB happens to sweep through the well's own exact cell at ANY speed would satisfy a pure overlap-
based capture check, destroying a hard-falling or cannon-launched object exactly like a slow one —
the opposite of "redirects" for the fast case. `checkGravityWellContacts(actor)` (the non-player
half, called unconditionally from `stepActor`'s non-player branch — it does its own temperament
gate internally since its scope differs from the switch/goal/lethal checks beside it) and
`checkPlayerTileContacts`'s own new `gravitywell` branch (the player half) both check
`Math.hypot(vx, vy) < GRAVITY_WELL_CAPTURE_SPEED` at the moment of overlapping the well's own cell
— below it, captured; at or above it (a hard fall, a bounce, a throw, a cannon shot), just the
ambient redirect from the stronger pull, already computed every frame regardless.

**Scope: `PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS`, deliberately NOT `isForceSusceptible` — the one place in this
patch that reads as inconsistent with its own neighbor and isn't.** `isForceSusceptible` exists
specifically to carve Large Stone OUT of every other optional force (wind/fan/magnet/bounce/
cannon); `checkGravityWellContacts` reads `PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS.includes(temperament) ||
isSteeringCharacter(temperament)` instead — the plain, un-narrowed list — because the request's own
wording was unqualified ("all moveable objects including all characters," no exception named), and
a black hole crushing even the "heavy" piece reads as consistent with its own premise, not a
contradiction of Large Stone's established identity elsewhere. This was the one real bug caught
during testing: an early draft used `isForceSusceptible` by reflex (it's the established gate for
"does this participate in an optional force"), which silently made Large Stone immune to the well
too, contradicting the design decision above — caught directly by a Playwright test that placed a
Large Stone in the well's pull and found it never got destroyed, not assumed correct from the code
alone. Deliberately excludes `stationary`/`collectible` — the same boundary every other optional
force already draws, since neither has a driven physics body to begin with (see
`isForceSusceptible`'s own comment) — and the new Moving Platform, which needs no explicit
exclusion at all: its own `stepActor` branch returns before this check is ever reached, the same
"too heavy/self-driven" identity Large Stone has toward every OTHER force, just arrived at for free
by construction rather than an added check. Flag widening either boundary to the user before doing
it — destroying the project's own last live collectible this way would also trigger a full-
collection win via `maybeRoundCompleteFromCollectibles`, a real side effect nobody's asked for yet.

**Consequences, exactly as specified, needing three separate reaction shapes, not one.** A plain
physics object (pushable/Large Stone/hazard ball/a grabbed-then-dropped carryable) is destroyed —
`triggerGravityWellCapture`, mirroring `destroyPhysicsObjectInLethal`'s own established shape
exactly (`destroyed` flag, splat sound/`popVanish` recipe, delayed `removeActorById`). An NPC
(wanderer/chaser/fleer) is sent back to its own `spawnX`/`spawnY` — in BOTH Build and Play mode,
per the request's own explicit wording — same function, branching on `isSteeringCharacter`. The
player needed its own dedicated function, `triggerPlayerGravityWellCapture`, rather than reusing
`triggerLethalHazard` (investigated first, not assumed): that function has NO positional-reset
logic in EITHER mode — only an instant Play-mode game-over — which can't satisfy the Build-mode
"sent back to the start" requirement the user explicitly asked for. The new function mirrors
`triggerLethalHazard`'s Play-mode shape exactly (same splat sound/recipe, same delayed game-over
stinger so it doesn't bury the animation) and adds the one thing that function never had: Build
mode repositions to `startPos`, the same field `resetActorsToSpawn` already uses for "the player's
own reset-to-start point." "Destroys" is read as the GENERAL rule the request states, with the
player and NPCs as its own two explicitly-named exceptions — you can't really delete the player or
a persistent NPC instance the way you can an inanimate object.

**A real test-authoring trap worth recording, the same shape this file has hit before**: a first
pass at the Build-mode player-capture test polled every 150ms and checked for the player's position
landing within 2px of `startPos`, and never saw it — not because the reset wasn't firing, but
because `GRAVITY_WELL_FORCE`'s own huge magnitude re-accelerates the player away from a fresh reset
within a handful of frames, so a slow, coarse poll essentially never lands during the brief window
it's actually sitting at `startPos`. Confirmed by polling every 20ms instead and watching
`hazardCooldown` (the capture's own re-trigger guard) spike back up to its full value — the
unambiguous signature of the reaction firing again — then checking position at exactly that moment,
which did land on `startPos` precisely. A separate test-authoring mistake hit the same "friction
alone defeats a naively-placed test" gotcha this file's own v2.28 section already documents for a
pushable's zero self-driven accel: a first "fast pass, should redirect not capture" test placed a
pushable far from the well with a high starting velocity, and friction (`FRICTION_DECEL = 1800`)
fully killed that velocity within about 100px of travel — well short of the well's own pull radius
— before the well ever got a chance to redirect anything, reading as "nothing happened" rather than
revealing the actual mechanism. Fixed the same way that section's own lesson prescribes: teleport
the object to already be well within the pull radius immediately before giving it velocity, and
sample fast, right away.

**Export pipeline**: `MOVING_PLATFORM_KIND`/`MOVING_PLATFORM_DEF_ID`/`MOVING_PLATFORM_RANGE_TILES`/
`MOVING_PLATFORM_SPEED` and `GRAVITY_WELL_RADIUS_TILES`/`GRAVITY_WELL_CAPTURE_SPEED` each got their
own `constantsSource` lines (`MOVING_PLATFORM_ART` needed none — it's baked into
`actorDefs[MOVING_PLATFORM_DEF_ID].art`, shipped wholesale, same as Large Stone's own art before
it); `checkGravityWellContacts`/`triggerGravityWellCapture`/`triggerPlayerGravityWellCapture` were
added to `runtimeFunctions`. `stepActor`/`shovePushables`/`checkPlayerTileContacts`/
`radialForceAccelFor`/`resetActorsToSpawn` were all already shared into the export from earlier
patches, so the platform's motion/immovability and the well's new pull/capture both apply
automatically to an exported game — confirmed directly rather than assumed: a fresh export's HTML
contains every new identifier, and a Moving Platform genuinely oscillates when the exported file is
actually played.

Verified with Playwright across several focused passes (31 targeted checks, plus the existing
8-sample regression sweep and two export tests): the platform defaults to horizontal and its
measured x-range matches `spawnX ± 4 tiles` exactly; clicking it toggles the axis both ways; it's
never displaced by a crate shoved into it (the crate stops at its edge instead) while still being
immovable-but-standable; a rider resting on top is genuinely carried along as the platform drifts
(true riding, confirmed via the pre-existing riding mechanism, not a new one); a slow/stationary
pushable drifting into the well is fully destroyed; a fast-moving pushable passing near the well is
NOT destroyed and shows a large, measured swing in its velocity vector (confirmed genuinely
redirected, not just "survived"); the player is instantly game-over'd when captured in Play mode;
the player is repositioned to `startPos` when captured in Build mode (confirmed via the
hazardCooldown-spike detection method above); a wanderer NPC is sent back to its own spawn point in
BOTH Build and Play mode, never destroyed; Large Stone IS captured/destroyed by the well despite
being immune to every other force; the full 8-sample regression sweep and two fresh exports (one
general, one specifically scenes with a Moving Platform + Gravity Well) all run error-free; and a
final debug-hook-free sanity pass (`grep -n "toyDebug" index.html` returning nothing) confirms no
test-only code was left behind.

## Sprite editor tool: up to 8 animation frames per sprite

Direct request on the standalone sprite editor (`prototypes/sprite-editor.html`, committed since
v2.36 — see that section above for why it lives in this repo at all): "let's create the ability to
add frames (up to 8) to create custom animations... three buttons, new frame, duplicate frame,
delete frame, and... a reference area where you can select between the frames. Also a preview for
the animation." Framed explicitly by the user as groundwork for a possible future game feature, not
a request to wire anything into `index.html` itself — scoped to the standalone tool only, same as
every other sprite-editor-only change this doc already covers (v2.27's Export-as-JS, v2.30's
new-sprite-creation/PNG-import). No debug hook, no `pat()`-native index.html integration, no
backward-compat migration inside `index.html` — none of that applies here, per the tool's own
established "not folded into this project's conventions" status.

**Data model**: `state[id]` (every sprite's live art) changes from a single flat 64-entry array to
an ARRAY of up to `MAX_FRAMES` (8) flat arrays — a static sprite is simply a 1-frame "animation,"
not a special case. `currentFrameIndex[id]` tracks which frame is currently loaded into the pixel
editor for that sprite; it's deliberately NOT persisted across a Save/Load round trip (every loaded
sprite starts back on frame 1) — a simplicity choice, not an oversight. `markDirty`'s comparison
generalizes for free: comparing `state[id]` against `[ORIGINAL_ART[id]]` (the original wrapped as a
1-frame animation) catches an edited pixel AND an added/removed frame with the same check, no
separate frame-count comparison needed.

**New Frame / Duplicate Frame / Delete Frame** insert or remove a frame right after whichever one
is currently selected (so the new/duplicated frame becomes the one you're editing next), disabled
at the 8-frame ceiling or the 1-frame floor respectively — a sprite can never have zero frames.
**The frame strip** (`#frameStrip`, left of the palette block) is the "reference area to select
between frames" the request asked for: one small thumbnail per frame, click to load it into "Your
edit"; painting and Import PNG both only ever touch whichever frame is currently selected there, so
building an 8-frame walk cycle means switching frames and drawing each one in turn. **Preview** is
a third canvas column (alongside the existing Reference/Your-edit pair) that loops through every
frame of the selected sprite on a fixed 300ms timer, reading live from `state[selectedId]` every
tick — a mid-edit pixel change shows up in the preview the next time its frame comes around, with
no restart needed; switching to a DIFFERENT sprite does reset the preview back to frame 1, so a
sprite's loop always starts from the beginning rather than wherever the previous sprite's cycle
happened to be.

**Export as JS gains a second shape, modeled on an existing index.html convention, not invented
from nothing.** A single-frame sprite still exports exactly as before (`const NAME_ART =
pat(...);`). A multi-frame sprite exports one `pat()` call per frame (`NAME_FRAME1_ART`,
`NAME_FRAME2_ART`, ...) plus `const NAME_FRAMES = [NAME_FRAME1_ART, ...];` tying them together —
mirroring how `index.html` already names its own animated-tile frame sets (`FIRE_FRAMES`,
`LAVA_FRAMES`), so pasting the result in reads consistently with what's already there rather than
introducing a competing naming scheme. This is the one part of this patch that changes the tool's
own *output format*, which per the v2.36 section above is the one case that does need a CLAUDE.md
note even for a sprite-editor-only change.

**Save JSON / Load JSON**: saving needed no code change at all — it already serializes `state`
wholesale, and a nested array of frames serializes exactly as cleanly as the old flat one did.
Loading gained real backward-compat logic: a loaded entry whose first element is itself an array is
the new multi-frame shape (used as-is, clamped to 8 frames); a loaded entry that's a bare 64-long
flat array is a save from before frames existed, wrapped as a single-frame animation — the exact
"older data predates a feature, re-derive a sane default" shape this whole project's own
`restoreProjectSnapshot` backward-compat sections already use repeatedly, applied here to this
tool's own, much smaller save format.

**A real test-fixture bug caught while verifying Import PNG, worth recording so it isn't rediscovered
the hard way again**: a commonly-copied "known good" 1×1 base64 PNG test fixture decodes with
alpha=127 once drawn through a canvas, not 255 — which this tool's own Import PNG logic correctly
treats as transparent per its already-documented "alpha under 50% imports as erased" rule. The
first attempt at testing Import PNG against a currently-selected frame read this as the feature
being broken (nothing ever got written); tracing the actual pixel data (not just the end result)
showed the import logic was working exactly as designed on a genuinely semi-transparent test image.
Fixed by generating a real, fully-opaque test PNG via canvas rather than trusting a copied base64
literal — the lesson generalizes to any future PNG-import test on this tool.

Verified with Playwright (46 checks): a fresh/original sprite starts at exactly 1 frame with Delete
disabled; New Frame inserts a blank frame right after the current one and selects it, leaving every
other frame untouched; Duplicate Frame does the same but copies the source frame's content;
clicking a frame thumbnail loads that frame for editing; New/Duplicate Frame correctly disable at
the 8-frame cap and clicking a disabled button is a genuine no-op; Delete Frame correctly disables
again once back down to 1 frame; the Preview canvas visibly cycles through more than one distinct
frame color over real elapsed time and immediately shows a newly-selected sprite's own frame 1;
Save/Load round-trips a multi-frame sprite exactly; loading a simulated pre-frames (bare flat-array)
save correctly wraps it as a single frame; Import PNG writes only into the currently-selected frame,
leaving every other frame of that sprite untouched; Export as JS produces a plain `NAME_ART` for a
single-frame edit and a full `NAME_FRAME1_ART..NAME_FRAME3_ART` + `NAME_FRAMES` set for a 3-frame
custom sprite, confirmed by actually executing the generated source via `new Function()` (with a
faithful local reimplementation of index.html's own `pat()`, since the tool itself never defines
it) and checking the reproduced frame colors match exactly, in order.

## Object-to-object interaction matrix: bomb chains, impact detonation, and a lot of corrected assumptions

The physics-unification patch fixed object-to-*force* coverage (does X respond to wind/bounce/
cannon/gravity). This is the other half: object-to-*object* — does one mechanism's effect become
another mechanism's trigger. The request was a matrix (rows = active mechanisms, columns = what they
could act on) with each cell marked ✓ (should already work), ? (genuinely unverified), or NEW
(doesn't exist, worth adding), plus three named priorities: check magnet's target list first,
add bomb chain reactions, add collision-triggered bomb detonation, and confirm in-flight objects keep
obeying every force. **The single most useful finding is that the matrix itself was wrong in several
places** — every ✓/? cell was checked directly (Playwright, not code-reading alone), and the
corrections below matter as much as the two new features.

**The "concrete gap, check first": magnet's pull list was ALREADY fixed — no code change.** The
request's prime suspect was `radialForceAccelFor`'s `magnetable` still being a separately maintained
list. It isn't: it already reads `isForceSusceptible(temperament) || temperament === "collectible" ||
isSteeringCharacter(temperament)` (the NPC-physics patch did this). Confirmed by test: a magnet
pulls a hazard ball, a dropped bomb actor, and a wandering NPC. **Correction to the matrix's "Magnet
→ NPC: probably not — would fight steering AI":** it does pull NPCs, and the `displacedCooldown`
recovery window is exactly the mechanism that resolves the "fights steering" worry — but only when the
pull is strong: `NPC_DISPLACEMENT_FORCE_THRESHOLD` (400px/s²) means a wanderer more than ~2.5 tiles
from a magnet (force ~200 at that range) is NOT displaced and its own steering simply wins. A first
test at ~3 tiles read "magnet doesn't pull NPCs" for exactly this reason; the real behavior is "pulls
when close enough to beat steering," verified at point-blank range with the wander heading pinned the
opposite way (peak vx toward the magnet 160, steering suppressed true).

**Magnet → Player: deliberately NOT implemented, flagged for the user's own decision.** The matrix
marked it "? (dangerous, delightful if yes)," but "a magnet attracts objects, not the player" is a
long-standing, repeatedly stated boundary (see v2.21's Magnet bullet: "never the player"). Reversing
it is a design-principle call, not a gap fix — raise it with the user before changing `magnetable`.

**NEW: bomb chain reactions.** `detonateBombAt`'s cell loop now treats a *neighboring bomb tile*
(`isBombGeometry`) as a chain trigger — `cancelBombArm` + a recursive `detonateBombAt` — instead of
leaving it untouched like any other non-solid neighbor. Naturally bounded: the bomb's own cell is
first in the `cells` array and is nulled before any neighbor's recursive call could see it again.
The actor loop gets the actor-world twin: a dropped/thrown bomb2 actor (`isCarryBombKind`) inside the
blast radius calls `detonateCarriedBombAt` instead of taking ordinary knockback. Two supporting
changes: the actor loop iterates `targetActors.slice()` (a chained detonation can mutate the array
mid-iteration) and skips any actor with `detonating` set; and `detonateCarriedBombAt` has a
`bombActor.detonating` re-entrancy guard — set *before* its own `detonateBombAt` call — which
centralizes double-detonation protection for every path that can reach it (fuse timeout, chain
reaction, lethal contact) rather than each call site needing its own check. Blast radius is unchanged
(cardinal-adjacent, radius 1): a bomb two cells away is NOT touched (tested).

**NEW: collision-triggered bomb detonation.** In `resolveAxis`'s existing bomb/cannon trigger block, a
physics object or steering NPC (`isPhysicsObject || isSteeringNPC`) that hits a bomb *tile* at
`speedAtImpact >= BOMB_IMPACT_DETONATE_SPEED` (240, just above the player's 220 run cap — the same
margin `GRAVITY_WELL_CAPTURE_SPEED` uses) detonates it immediately. `speedAtImpact` is captured
*before* the axis branch zeroes/reflects velocity (a snapshot taken afterward would always read
~0 or the reflected value). The player's own touch is unchanged — it still arms a fuse regardless of
speed — and an ordinary slow shove into a bomb still just stops at its edge (tested both ways).
Hazard balls bounce off every wall unconditionally, but the detonation check is independent of the
bounce, so a fast hazard ball both reflects *and* detonates.

**Large Stone's missing sticky exemption — fixed.** The matrix expected Sticky → Large Stone to be
"exempt (heavy)"; it wasn't, because `isStuckToSticky`/`isOnStickyGround` are fully kind-agnostic and
sticky had never been gated per-kind. `isLargeStone` is now computed earlier in `stepActor` and gates
both `touchingSticky` and `stickyMoveMult`. Tested with a control: a Large Stone pressed into a
sticky wall keeps falling (270px), while an ordinary crate in the identical setup is frozen (0px).

**Lethal row — three matrix ✓s were false, because no such reactions existed.** An exhaustive grep
found `triggerLethalHazard` is player-only (three call sites) and `checkPhysicsObjectLethalContacts`
was `PUSHABLE_KIND`-only. So NPC, hazard ball, and bomb-vs-lethal had *no* reaction at all.
Resolved as: hazard ball → destroyed (same as a crate); bomb actor → **detonates** via
`detonateCarriedBombAt` rather than silently vanishing (the matrix's own open question — detonating
is the more game-like answer); NPC → new `checkNpcLethalContacts`/`triggerNpcLethalReset`, sent back to
its own `spawnX`/`spawnY` (never destroyed), mirroring `triggerGravityWellCapture`'s NPC branch
exactly. `destroyPhysicsObjectInLethal`'s log text is now generic ("Destroyed by lethal!") since it
no longer only ever means a crate. Still deliberately excluded: Large Stone (immune by design),
magnet/trampoline/cannon2 (unasked either way — left as an acknowledged gap, not silently widened),
and **collectible** — destroying the last live collectible would trigger a full-collection win via
`maybeRoundCompleteFromCollectibles`, the same side-effect risk already flagged for the gravity well.

**Plate vs Button: the matrix's single "Plate/Button" row was really two different functions.**
`updatePressurePlates` has *zero* temperament filtering — every overlapping actor triggers it, so
Plate already works for Large Stone, bomb actors, hazard balls, collectibles, everything (the matrix's
"not currently"/"n/a" labels for Plate were wrong). Button (`checkPhysicsObjectSwitchContacts`) was
`CARRYABLE_KINDS`-only, so a hazard ball or NPC resting on one did nothing. Its call-site gate in
`stepActor` is now `PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS.includes(t) || isSteeringCharacter(t)` — deliberately **not**
`isForceSusceptible`, which would have *removed* Large Stone (already working) from Button's scope.
The shared call site was split in two so **Goal's gate stays `CARRYABLE_KINDS`-only** — a wandering NPC
winning by stumbling onto a goal tile was never the ask (the Catapult Factory's premise depends on it).

**Collectibles are fully inert — the matrix's Gravity → Collectible "✓" and the whole Collectible
column's "?"s collapse to "n/a".** `stepActor`'s `stationary`/`collectible` branch applies no
gravity, wind, sticky, bounce, or magnet — it only decays residual velocity. A collectible placed
mid-air under gravity does not fall (tested: 0.0px over 800ms). `magnetable`'s `|| temperament ===
"collectible"` is therefore dead code (already noted in the unification section). Making collectibles
physical is a real design change with win-condition consequences, not a bug fix; not attempted.

**Deliberately preserved, flagged: a bomb blast still knocks back Large Stone.** The matrix expected
"exempt (heavy)," but `detonateBombAt`'s knockback loop has never type-filtered anything, with an
explicit existing comment ("a bomb doesn't discriminate between the player and an NPC"). A point-blank
explosion moving even a heavy object reads as plausible (unlike a gentle magnet or steady wind), so
this was left as-is rather than silently resolved either way.

**In-flight objects keep obeying every force — confirmed, no bug.** A cannon-launched crate flying
through a south-wind corridor in free-roam (where wind is the *only* possible source of southward
motion) drifted ~300px south past its own launch apex. Forces are integrated every frame regardless
of how an object got its velocity.

**Other matrix cells confirmed directly:** Sticky clings a pushable, hazard ball, bomb actor, and NPC
alike (all frozen, 0.0px) — only Large Stone is exempt; Button/Large Stone already worked.

**Test-authoring traps hit (all the same shape this file has recorded before):** (1) `enterPlayMode`
runs `resetActorsToSpawn`, which zeroes any velocity set *before* Play — set test velocity *after*
entering Play and waiting out the READY pause. (2) A non-player actor's velocity is clamped to
`NPC_MAX_SPEED` (160) the next frame unless `bouncyCooldown` is also set — a raw `vx = 500` test
silently became 160 and read as "below the detonation threshold." (3) `placeActorDef`-style
center-based placement left the actor ~5px short of the wall; use explicit `x`/`y` so the AABB already
overlaps the target and the first frame's `resolveAxis` snaps it flush (friction otherwise eats a
non-steering actor's velocity within ~3px). (4) A test actor def with `art: null` crashes
`drawArtScaled` on the first render, which halts the whole `frame()` loop — every actor in the scene
then looks frozen, producing false "doesn't move/doesn't react" failures for *unrelated* actors; give
test defs a real 64-entry art array. (5) A wanderer's heading is random every 0.8-1.7s, so net
displacement over a long window is noise — pin the heading and sample peak velocity instead.
(6) A cannon fires a fixed direction; approaching from the side it fires toward self-collides and
zeroes (see Catapult Factory) — approach from the opposite side. (7) A free-roam launch decays under
friction within ~250ms, so a wind corridor must cover the *whole area the launch arcs through*, not
just the launch row. (8) Exporting within ~220ms of an actor being destroyed in Build mode bakes
`destroyed: true` into the export (no timer ever removes it, and every lethal check early-returns on
the flag) — a real but very narrow edge (it pre-dates this patch for crates); wait out the fade
before exporting in a test.

**Export pipeline**: `BOMB_IMPACT_DETONATE_SPEED` got a `constantsSource` line;
`checkNpcLethalContacts`/`triggerNpcLethalReset` were added to `runtimeFunctions`.
`detonateCarriedBombAt`/`detonateBombAt`/`checkPhysicsObjectLethalContacts` were already shared.

Verified with Playwright (14 + 26 + 8 targeted checks): tile chain (adjacent bomb detonates, a bomb
two cells away doesn't), actor-in-blast chain (marked `detonating` immediately, removed after the blast
animation), double-trigger re-entrancy (removed exactly once), fast hazard ball detonates a bomb tile
while a slow shoved crate doesn't, the sticky/Large Stone pair with its control, Button vs hazard
ball/NPC/Large Stone, Lethal vs hazard ball/bomb actor/NPC, magnet vs hazard ball/NPC/bomb actor,
in-flight wind redirection, sticky vs hazard ball/bomb/NPC, collectible-doesn't-fall; the full
8-sample sweep (each loaded through the real samples modal, played ~3s held-right, zero errors); an
export of The Catapult Factory played error-free; and a functional export test (a hazard ball in
lethal is destroyed, adjacent bombs chain-detonate, in the exported file itself). The debug hook was
stripped (`grep -n "toyDebug" index.html` returns nothing).

## Gravity Well becomes the animated "Black Hole" (art + palette name only)

Direct request: "Replace art and renames current gravity well," with a user-supplied 6-frame `pat()`
sprite ("Black Hole"). Every earlier section of this doc that says "Gravity Well" / `gravitywell` is
describing this same object; nothing about it was split or duplicated.

**Scope: art and kid-facing name only, mechanics untouched.** The internal geometry id stays
`"gravitywell"` (board serialization, `FIXTURE_GEOMETRIES`, `checkGravityWellContacts`,
`triggerGravityWellCapture`, `GRAVITY_WELL_*` constants all unchanged — same "don't churn an id nobody
but the code sees" call as the crate/Gate/cannon2 renames). The palette-facing name changes through the
established mechanism, `GEOMETRY_LABELS.gravitywell = "black hole"`, which the Fixtures row already
reads for both the swatch title and its on-screen label. The in-game log lines for being captured now say
"black hole" too (kids read those); internal comments and identifiers keep saying "gravity well".
`CAPTION_TEXT.gravitywell` already described it as "A black hole..." and needed no change.

**Animation: wall-clock frame cycling, no per-tile state.** `BLACK_HOLE_FRAME1_ART`..`FRAME6_ART` plus
`BLACK_HOLE_FRAMES` are the user's art verbatim; `BLACK_HOLE_FRAME_MS` (130, a tuning choice — flag if
it reads too fast/slow) picks the frame as `floor(performance.now() / BLACK_HOLE_FRAME_MS) % 6`, the
same stateless approach `currentSkinFrame` already uses for animated ground skins, in a new
`gravitywell` branch in `drawGameplayTile`. `GEOMETRY_ICONS.gravitywell` (the old static ring art is
deleted) is frame 1, so the palette swatch is a still image — the swatch row doesn't animate, only
placed tiles do, same as every other animated tile here.

**Export pipeline**: one new `constantsSource` line ships `BLACK_HOLE_FRAMES`/`BLACK_HOLE_FRAME_MS`
(`GEOMETRY_ICONS` is exported wholesale, but the frames array isn't part of it).
`drawGameplayTile` was already in `runtimeFunctions`.

**No backward-compat work needed**: ids and board strings are unchanged, so every old save/sample
renders the new art automatically.

Verified with Playwright through the real UI (no debug hook): the Fixtures swatch is titled/labelled
"black hole" (no leftover "gravitywell" title), uses the new purple/black palette, and the hover
caption reads correctly; a painted tile renders 6 distinct frames over ~1.4s; an exported game
containing a painted black hole contains both new constants and animates it (6 distinct frames in
~1.1s) with zero errors; the full 8-sample sweep and the standard export test pass. The
capture/pull mechanics were not re-traced since nothing they read changed beyond log-string text.

## Sound polish: a quieter master bus and a voice limiter (no more stacking)

Direct feedback, two parts: (1) sound is "very loud" next to other websites; (2) sounds stack into
an annoying drone — standing on a bouncy tile retriggered `boing` every few frames with every copy
playing out in full (~0.47s each), and a spawner's pile of chasers sitting in one spot did the same
with catch/bump sounds. Both fixes live in the two playback functions, so every reaction in both
engines gets them with no per-call-site changes.

**Loudness.** Every chip design is normalized to peak 0.7 and `playChip` connected it straight to
`ctx.destination`. Both engines now connect to `getMasterBus()` instead: a gain of `MASTER_VOLUME`
(0.3, about -10 dB — a judgment call; it's the one knob to retune) into a `DynamicsCompressor` used
as a safety limiter (threshold -14 dB, ratio 12, fast attack), so a legitimate pile-up of
*different* sounds can't spike either. The legacy engine's relative balance to the chip engine is
unchanged (its peak was already lower); both just sit under the same master.

**Stacking.** `claimVoice(key, durationMs)` is the gate `playChip` (key = design name) and
`playPatch` (key = `patch:<id>`) pass before creating any audio nodes. Per key it enforces a minimum
gap between starts and a cap on simultaneous copies (`SOUND_LIMITS`, default `SOUND_LIMIT_DEFAULT`
= 80ms / 2 copies; boing and trampolineBoing 250ms / 1 copy, so a settling bounce is a steady
boing every ~0.5s at most instead of a pile; splat 1 copy), plus `SOUND_MAX_TOTAL` (10) live voices
overall. A second copy that is allowed to overlap plays at `SOUND_OVERLAP_GAIN` (0.55). A refused
trigger is simply dropped — the copy already playing covers it, nothing is queued. Different sounds
never block each other (boing + splat + kaboom in one tick all play). This deliberately throttles by
sound *name*, not by source actor, since the complaint was the aggregate noise.

**Export pipeline**: `getMasterBus`/`claimVoice` added to `runtimeFunctions`; one `constantsSource`
line ships `MASTER_VOLUME`/`SOUND_LIMIT_DEFAULT`/`SOUND_LIMITS`/`SOUND_MAX_TOTAL`/
`SOUND_OVERLAP_GAIN`; `masterBus` and `activeVoices` declared by hand in the export template next to
`audioCtx`.

Verified in Chromium by hooking Web Audio: the bus builds once (0.3 gain + one compressor);
60 boings over ~0.6s start 2 voices (was 60); 40 same-tick thuds start 1; legacy-engine spam is
capped; three different sounds in one tick all play; a sound plays again after finishing; finished
voices are pruned; an exported game builds the limiter bus and plays with zero errors; 8-sample
sweep and export test clean. Not covered: actually listening — loudness is a number picked
by judgment and may want one more nudge. Standing on a bouncy tile was verified at the sound layer
(the throttle), not by scripting a physical bounce.

## Heart, Key, Star — the three power-up collectibles, and a stricter collection win (v2.44)

(The patch note called itself v2.42; CLAUDE.md already uses v2.42/v2.42.1 and informally refers to
"v2.43-era" for the READY! pause, so this is labeled v2.44 — see "Version labels are not reliable".)

Heart, Key and Star have existed as collectible art since v2.27 (`colHeart`/`colKey`/`colStar` in
`COLLECTIBLE_SEEDS`) and did nothing but pop. They now have behavior, all built from systems that
already existed; the only new state is `spareHearts`, `starTimer`, `keyChannelsUnlocked` and a
per-key `channel`. No scripting layer, no new temperament: `POWERUP_DEF_IDS` is the one small closed
list that tells them apart from cheese/gem/coin.

**The win rule changed first.** Collecting every ordinary collectible wins ONLY if the game has no
goal tile. `gameHasGoalTile()` is game-wide (any room's board has geometry `goal`) because every
room's win condition already doubles as the game's win (`roundComplete`) — per-room would let a
goal-less side room end a game whose real win is a goal elsewhere. `maybeRoundCompleteFromCollectibles`
is guarded with it, so Build and Play agree; `triggerGoal` is untouched. `countCollectibles()` excludes
`POWERUP_DEF_IDS`, and power-up pickup (`collectPowerup`) never calls the collection check at all —
otherwise a heart in a room with no ordinary collectibles (count already 0) would win instantly.
Sample audit: only The Monster's Den and The Gravity Switcheroo have both a goal and ordinary
collectibles — both now win by goal only (Monster's Den's goal is the intended win and is reachable;
Switcheroo's collectibles are now just points, which also removes the old bait-gem workaround's
reason to exist but is harmless). Cheese Heist has NO goal tile, so collection still wins there
(confirmed by collecting the last cheese in a real Play session). The other five have goals and no
ordinary collectibles — unaffected.

**Heart = a hit buffer on top of lives.** One always-full base heart plus up to `MAX_SPARE_HEARTS`
(3) spares, Play-only, reset on Play start/restart (via `clearTransientRuntimeState`) and on game
over. Every non-lethal hit goes through `takePlayerHit(source)`: star active -> nothing; a spare ->
spend it (no life lost, no reset to start, `HIT_INVULN_MS` 1.2s flicker window); otherwise
`consumeLifeIfPlaying()` exactly as before. Callers keep their own side effects: `triggerHazard`
bails before cooldown/knockback when starred or flickering and otherwise does its usual yelp+flash+
knockback (a falling-crate crush reaches it too); the chaser-catch branch plays the same `caughtPunt`
but, when a spare absorbs it, skips the reset and shoves the player clear instead. **Deliberate
deviation from the note**: the flicker window is a new `actor.invulnT`, not `hazardCooldown`.
`triggerLethalHazard` gates on `hazardCooldown`, so reusing it would have made a lethal tile touched
during the flicker harmless — and "lethal ignores hearts" is the whole point of the tile. Lethal is
otherwise untouched (full hearts + lava = instant game over, verified). A Heart at the cap is ignored
and stays on the floor. Build has no hearts: it pops with its sound and Reset restores it. HUD: the
old `❤ 3` lives readout is now `Lives 3` (so two red-heart readouts don't sit side by side) next to a
heart row (base + spares; spending one plays a CSS pop on a ghost heart).

**Key = a permanent button you pick up.** A key carries a per-actor `channel` (set from the shared
channel color picker in `placeNewActor`; old saves without one default to the first color, red).
Picking it up sets `keyChannelsUnlocked[color]` — a third source in `isChannelOn`, next to
`switchChannels` and `plateChannelsActive` — so every same-color gate opens with its existing ghost
